## D-PRI-001
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-002-A answered.
- User answer: "one configured currency per vendor—RSD or BAM—with the currency preserved on each order and no automatic conversion."
- Rationale: Single-currency vendor trading with stable historical order currency.
- Implications: R-PRI-001. Catalogue/terms operate in the vendor's configured RSD or BAM; each order preserves its currency independently of future configuration changes. No currency-conversion engine/provider is in scope. Replaces the old frontend proposal for deployment-wide BAM.
- Follow-up: D-CTR-005 resolves currency by country: Serbia RSD, Bosnia and Herzegovina BAM. Exceptional correction and open-cart/terms handling remain Q-PRI-002-B; decimal precision, rounding, tax and serialization remain Q-PRI-002/003.
- No exchange rates or automatic reinterpretation of historical amounts are introduced.

## D-LOC-001
- P0.34 refinement: D-CAT-071–080, D-ORG-036–040 and D-LOC-002–006 resolve CSV fields/format/byte bound/preview lifecycle, image animation/output/reference policy, saved contacts/instructions and launch language/preference/fallback choices. Earlier remaining notes below apply only where still unresolved.
- Status: APPROVED requirement plus DESIGN SELECTED UNDER EXPLICIT USER DELEGATION — architecture portion of Q-LOC-001-A.
- User answer: "We should have a system where including new lanaguges and dialects is easy. It doesn't have to be backends task, we can deffer this to frontend or some external configuration services. Or we can keep it backend. Whatever you think it's best."
- Delegation boundary: The user authorized choosing localization placement/approach. This is not general authorization to choose other unresolved business or engineering details.
- Selected design: Each rendering layer owns its translations: frontend owns UI copy; backend owns text it sends itself (email/push and other server-generated human text). API business errors expose stable codes and structured parameters for client rendering rather than requiring server-translated UI prose. Use extensible locale identifiers and versioned resource bundles plus an explicit supported-locale/fallback configuration. Adding a language/dialect adds resources/configuration; it must not require a new enum value, schema migration or domain-rule branch.
- Selected delivery approach: Keep translation resources/configuration version-controlled with their owning application initially; no runtime translation/configuration service is required. A future translation platform can export those resources without changing the domain contract. This choice does not assign frontend implementation to us.
- Rationale: UI and outbound messages have different renderers; resource-based configuration keeps language additions routine without coupling backend rules to UI translations or introducing a new runtime service.
- Implications: R-LOC-001. Backend locale preference uses a configurable supported set, not a fixed two-language persistence enum. Country, currency and language remain distinct concepts. Exact preference ownership, initial locales, fallback mapping/default, resource format, library, message syntax and update/review workflow remain explicit decisions.
- Evidence: [W3C language-tag guidance](https://www.w3.org/International/questions/qa-choosing-language-tags.en) describes language/script/region distinctions; [Java ResourceBundle documentation](https://docs.oracle.com/en/java/javase/21/docs/api/java.base/java/util/ResourceBundle.html) demonstrates resource separation and locale fallback. Sources informed the design; Java 21 and ResourceBundle are not selected as project versions/dependencies.
- Remaining: D-LOC-002–006 settle launch languages, user preference, explicit setup default, English missing-template fallback and single-language authored catalogue content. Exact identifiers/resource conventions remain Q-LOC-001-C and preference/delivery lifecycle follows Batch 31. No assumption that regional tags alone encode every dialect.

- Remaining: Detailed permissions, country correction lifecycle, database enforcement mechanisms, schema and operational settings. Domestic-only order semantics and country topology are settled.

## D-CTR-001

## D-CTR-002
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CTR-002 answered.
- User answer: "A: Each organization belongs to one country; a business operating in both has separate organization records."

- Remaining: Organization legal fields/deduplication and country correction/change lifecycle (Q-CTR-002-A). The recommendation to lock country after trading was not explicitly selected.
- Releases: Organization-country ownership and domestic order consistency planning.

- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-001-B answered.

- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-001-C answered.

## D-CAT-009
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-003-B answered.

- Implications: R-CAT-009. Minimum and step are positive whole numbers, each defaulting to 1. Valid order quantities are minimum + n × step for an integer n >= 0. Reject fractions, quantities below minimum and step mismatches without rounding. The step is anchored at the minimum, as specified in the selected option.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-CAT-016 makes units vendor-defined; their model/validation, maximum quantities, numeric bounds and later rule-change effects remain open.
- Planning effect: Updated requirements, domain/API constraints and VS-03/04/09/11 verification. Detailed design and implementation remain gated.

- Implications: R-CAT-010. Pricing an unpriced product never implicitly activates it. Explicit activation requires current catalogue permission and valid pricing. A combined price-and-activation action requires both permissions; other eligibility checks remain applicable.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-002 settles positive entered prices and required positive base. D-PRI-009–011 settle monetary precision/rounding. Other validation, additional publication fields, transaction design and retained-price behavior after removal remain open; D-PRI-020 requires explicit deactivation before active-product base removal.

- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact restore fields, media/price/stock retention, atomic mechanics and simultaneous edits remain open. Restoration grants no price or stock editing authority.

- User answer (faithful summary): "B: Record the same information, but make the reason optional."

- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact event fields, history access/retention, no-op/failed-attempt audit, system initialization attribution and storage mechanics remain open. Do not enforce the unselected mandatory-reason proposal.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API rules, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; exact transition/transaction rules remain gated.

## D-CAT-043
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-006-E answered.
- User answer (faithful summary): "B: Allow disabling immediately; we then define how pending orders affect retained/reconciled counts."
- Implications: R-CAT-043. A stock-authorized actor may disable vendor tracking immediately even with outstanding orders whose stock was deducted. Pending orders do not block disabling, and retained counts/history are preserved. New orders while tracking is off are not stock-checked or deducted.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-CAT-045/046 select immediate off-mode credits and re-enable with older orders using net available counts while preserving deduction history. Concurrent toggle/order/cancellation/confirmation ordering remains open; no settlement of old deductions is selected.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API rules, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; exact transition/transaction rules remain gated.

## D-CAT-044
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-005-J answered.
- User answer (faithful summary): "A: Retain its count/history. If tracking is on, require stock-authorized confirmation/update before reactivating the product."
- Implications: R-CAT-044. Product archive retains stock count and history. With vendor tracking on, stock-authorized confirmation/update is required before product reactivation. Restore still checks capacity, returns inactive and needs explicit catalogue-authorized activation with other requirements satisfied.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact confirmation freshness, existing-order effects on archived products, tracking-off restoration and concurrent archive/count/activation rules remain open. Archive does not cancel existing orders or erase their deduction history.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API rules, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; exact transition/transaction rules remain gated.

## Outstanding orders across stock-tracking changes — 2026-10-13
Three selections approved; no implementation authorization. Off-mode cancellation credits are immediate, re-enable with older orders is allowed under confirmed net availability, and manual off-mode edits are permitted.

## D-CAT-045
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-006-F answered.
- User answer (faithful summary): "A: Immediately add them to the retained count, exactly once. Tracking stays off."
- Implications: R-CAT-045. A valid pre-dispatch cancellation of a previously stock-deducted order immediately restores its actual deduction to the retained count, once, even while tracking is off. No deferred credit queue or automatic re-enable is selected. Orders without original deductions receive no automatic credit.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact cancellation rights, returns (partial dispatch is excluded by D-ORD-005), concurrency, retries and history schema remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. Exact concurrency/contract conventions remain gated.

## D-CAT-046
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-006-G answered.
- User answer (faithful summary): "A: Allow it after confirming available counts that already exclude quantities committed to those orders. Previously deducted orders retain automatic cancellation credits. Orders placed while tracking was off receive no retroactive deduction or automatic credit; their later cancellation may require a manual adjustment."
- Implications: R-CAT-046. Allow tracking re-enable with older unfulfilled orders after stock-authorized confirmation/update of available counts net of all outstanding commitments. Preserve original deduction/credit obligations across reconciliation; do not settle or clear them on a new count. Off-mode orders remain outside automatic stock accounting, without retroactive deductions/credits; authorized manual adjustment may be needed if they later cancel. Missing-to-zero initialization under D-CAT-035 still applies.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact confirmation freshness, simultaneous submission/cancellation/count confirmation, auditing/DTO conventions remain open; D-ORD-005 excludes partial fulfillment. Example: confirming 5 available with a prior deducted order of 3 still pending, then validly cancelling it before dispatch, yields 8 once.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. Exact concurrency/contract conventions remain gated.

## D-CAT-047
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-005-K answered.
- User answer (faithful summary): "A: Yes, using the approved permissions, history and nonnegative-count rules. Re-enabling still requires confirmation/update."
- Implications: R-CAT-047. Allow stock-authorized absolute/delta edits to retained counts while tracking is off, enforcing whole nonnegative counts, change attribution and optional reason as already approved. Edits do not enable stock checking or waive required re-enable confirmation. Automatic cancellation credits follow D-CAT-045 independently.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Upper bounds, zero/no-op semantics, stale-write/retry conventions, confirmation freshness and exact event schema remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. Exact concurrency/contract conventions remain gated.

## Pricing calculation approvals — 2026-10-13
Five selections approved in Batch 20; these are business-rule approvals, not implementation authorization.

## D-PRI-002
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-A answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Require a base price before activation; base, volume-tier and buyer-specific prices must be greater than zero."
- Implications: R-PRI-002. Require a positive base price for activation and strictly positive manually entered base, tier and buyer-specific prices. Missing is not zero; catalogue-authorized incomplete unpriced preparation remains allowed. Adding pricing never activates automatically; combined writes require applicable grants.
- Acceptance example: Activation without base price fails even if an agreed buyer price exists; reject manually entered zero/negative base, tier and buyer-specific prices.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-008–011 approve promotional zero and precision; D-PRI-020 requires explicit deactivation before/together with active-product base removal. D-PRI-034 settles retained-price treatment; upper bounds and history remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, technical mechanism or UI behavior selected.

## D-PRI-003
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-B answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: The reached quantity threshold determines the price for every unit ordered."
- Implications: R-PRI-003. Use the unit price at the highest reached volume threshold for every unit of that product quantity in the order; use base when no tier is reached. No graduated brackets.
- Acceptance example: Base 100, threshold 10 at 90: quantity 9 totals 900; quantity 10 totals 900; quantity 12 totals 1080 before promotions/tax/fees.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-009–011/016–019 settle precision, decreasing tiers, valid distinct thresholds, duplicate-product rejection and invalidating base edits. D-PRI-033–036 settle quantity-rule coordination and baseless pricing preparation; exact validation/concurrency remains open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, technical mechanism or UI behavior selected.

## D-PRI-004
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-C answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Yes; the agreed price wins."
- Implications: R-PRI-004. A buyer-specific agreed price takes precedence over ordinary base/tier pricing even if higher. Do not compare them to choose the cheaper one.
- Acceptance example: Normal reached-tier unit price 90 and agreed buyer price 95 resolve to 95 before promotions.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-009–011 settle precision and D-PRI-021 permits at most one current agreed price per product/buyer without buyer tiers. Buyer-price lifecycle/eligibility, quote validity and wire/storage conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, technical mechanism or UI behavior selected.

## D-PRI-005
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-D answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Yes; apply the promotion after selecting the agreed price."
- Implications: R-PRI-005. Applicable promotions apply after selecting buyer-specific pricing; a negotiated price is not automatically exempt. If no buyer-specific price exists, apply promotions after ordinary base/tier resolution.
- Acceptance example: Agreed unit price 80 plus an eligible 10% promotion resolves to 72 before tax/fees.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-007–011 settle replacement-price semantics, promotional zero and monetary precision/rounding; D-PRI-022–029 settle targeting/scheduling direction and post-start freeze/permanent stop; exact time/target validation remains open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, technical mechanism or UI behavior selected.

## D-PRI-006
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-E answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Apply only the single promotion producing the lowest resulting price."
- Implications: R-PRI-006. Compare eligible promotion outcomes against the resolved pre-promotion price and apply only the one yielding the lowest price. Promotions never stack.
- Acceptance example: Resolved price 100 with eligible 10% and 20% promotions resolves to 80, not a stacked 72.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-007–015 settle replacement-price semantics, zero floor, monetary/percentage precision, no promotion increases and promotion-to-promotion ties. D-PRI-022–032 settle eligibility direction and no zero-saving attribution; exact snapshot fields and validation remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, technical mechanism or UI behavior selected.

## Promotion meaning and monetary precision — 2026-10-13
Five selections approved in Batch 21; no implementation authorization. Fixed promotions explicitly use replacement prices, overriding the earlier amount-off recommendation.

## D-PRI-007
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-A answered B, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "B: Replace the unit price with that amount."
- Implications: R-PRI-007. A fixed promotion supplies the final promotional price per sellable unit, rather than an amount to subtract. Evaluate it alongside eligible percentage promotions under the best-single-promotion rule.
- Acceptance example: Resolved 100 with fixed promotional price 20 yields 20, not 80.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-012 forbids promotion price increases; D-PRI-015 settles promotion-to-promotion ties. D-PRI-022/023/032 settle targeting and no zero-saving attribution; fixed-price bounds and exact projection rules remain open. Replacement semantics supersede frontend amount-off behavior.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; no implementation or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-008
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-B answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Yes. Allow zero; a discount exceeding the price stops at zero."
- Implications: R-PRI-008. Promotional results may be zero; floor negative calculated discounted results at zero. This does not permit zero base/tier/buyer-specific prices, negative promotion input, or otherwise invalid percentage values. Validation bounds remain separate.
- Acceptance example: A valid promotion resulting in zero is allowed; a negative calculated discount outcome is floored at zero. A fixed promotional price of zero is distinct from an invalid zero base price.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-013/014 approve percentage range and precision, rejecting above-100 input. Other input validation remains open. Fixed promotions use replacement semantics under D-PRI-007.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; no implementation or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-009
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-002-C answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Up to two, such as `12.34`."
- Implications: R-PRI-009. Entered base/tier/buyer-specific prices and fixed promotional prices have at most two decimal places. Reject excess input precision rather than silently rounding. Goods line amounts use two decimals. Percentage precision and tax/refund calculation remain separate.
- Acceptance example: Accept 12.34; reject monetary input 12.345 rather than save 12.35. Computed intermediate values may have more precision before final unit rounding.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Maximum amounts, wire/storage types and numeric normalization conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; no implementation or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-010
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-002-D answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Round the discounted unit price to two decimals, then multiply by quantity."
- Implications: R-PRI-010. Round each candidate final promotional unit price to two decimals, compare outcomes under the best-single rule, and multiply the selected rounded unit price by the whole quantity. Sum resulting goods line amounts for the goods subtotal. Do not round only after multiplying. Apply the zero floor before final rounding.
- Acceptance example: 0.05 per unit with 10% off gives 0.045, rounded to 0.05; quantity 3 totals 0.15, not 0.14. Distinct raw candidates that round equally follow D-PRI-015's creation/ID tie rule.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-015 settles ties between promotions after rounding; D-PRI-032 settles baseline equality as no applied promotion; tax/fees/refunds and exact historical snapshot fields remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; no implementation or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-011
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-002-E answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Round-half-up: `1.005 → 1.01`."
- Implications: R-PRI-011. Use round-half-up at the approved goods-unit-price rounding stage, with exact decimal semantics. No premature intermediate rounding or binary floating-point approximation may change the result; exact implementation/wire/storage types remain unselected.
- Acceptance example: Computed unit result 1.005 rounds to 1.01; 1.015 rounds to 1.02. Entered 1.005 remains invalid under D-PRI-009.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Tax and refund rounding remain separate decisions.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification; no implementation or frontend change authorized.

## Promotion validation and tier-price ordering — 2026-10-13
Five Batch 22 answers approved; no implementation authorization.

## D-PRI-012
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-C answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Never; ignore promotions priced above the resolved normal/agreed price."
- Implications: R-PRI-012. A promotion cannot increase the resolved pre-promotion unit price. Exclude candidates above the resolved base/tier or agreed buyer price, then choose the best remaining eligible promotion. If none remain, keep the resolved price. This does not alter agreed-price precedence over ordinary pricing.
- Acceptance example: Agreed price 80 and only fixed promotion 90 charges 80. A fixed promotion 70 may reduce it to 70.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-032 selects no promotion on equality; D-PRI-022–029 settle targeting/time and immutable started/permanently stopped definitions. Detailed validation/history remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-013
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-D answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Greater than 0%, up to and including 100%."
- Implications: R-PRI-013. Percentage discount input must be greater than zero and at most 100. Reject zero, negative and above-100 percentages; a 100% promotion produces an allowed zero result.
- Acceptance example: Reject 0%, -1% and 100.01%; accept 100% and charge zero when eligible.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Normalization and exact field/error conventions remain open; fixed-price input bounds are separate.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-014
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-E answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Up to two decimal places, such as `12.25%`."
- Implications: R-PRI-014. Percentage inputs allow at most two decimal places; reject excess precision instead of silently rounding. Combine with the approved greater-than-zero/at-most-100 range. Keep intermediate calculation precision until final unit half-up rounding.
- Acceptance example: Accept 12.25%; reject 12.345%; smallest positive value at this precision is 0.01%.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Wire/storage representation and normalization remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-015
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-F answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: The earliest-created promotion; use stable promotion ID ordering if creation times also match. Editing does not change creation order."
- Implications: R-PRI-015. When eligible promotions produce the same rounded unit price, attribute the result to the earliest-created promotion. If creation times match, use stable promotion ID order. Edits preserve creation order; no vendor-assigned priority is selected.
- Acceptance example: On resolved 100, a fixed promotion 90 and a 10% promotion both yield 90; whichever was created first is recorded. Equal timestamps use stable ID order. Editing the older promotion does not make it newer.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact ID comparison and timestamp representation remain technical choices. D-PRI-032 resolves baseline equality as no applied promotion; snapshot fields remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend change authorized.

## D-PRI-016
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-F answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Each tier must be cheaper than the base price and the preceding tier."
- Implications: R-PRI-016. In increasing quantity-threshold order, every tier price must be strictly below base and every preceding tier price, while remaining positive with approved precision. Equal or increasing tier prices are invalid.
- Acceptance example: Base 100, 10+ at 90, 20+ at 95 is invalid; 20+ at 90 is also invalid; 20+ at 80 is valid.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-017–020 settle valid distinct thresholds, duplicate-line rejection, invalidating base edits and explicit deactivation before base removal. D-PRI-033–036 settle quantity-rule coordination and retained-price preparation; technical conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend change authorized.

## Tier thresholds and pricing lifecycle — 2026-10-13
Five Batch 23 answers approved; no implementation authorization.

## D-PRI-017
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-G answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Each threshold must be an allowed order quantity strictly above the product’s minimum."
- Implications: R-PRI-017. Volume thresholds are distinct within each product and must be allowed whole order quantities strictly above its minimum, following minimum + n × step. Other products/vendors may reuse thresholds.
- Acceptance example: Minimum 5 and step 5 permit thresholds 10, 15 and 20, but not 5, 7 or fractional values.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-033 resolves invalidating minimum/step edits through explicit coordinated corrections; numeric bounds/concurrency remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend implementation authorized.

## D-PRI-018
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-H answered B, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "B: Reject duplicate product lines; require one line per product."
- Implications: R-PRI-018. Reject an order submission containing the same product more than once; require one line per product. Do not merge quantities or price duplicate lines independently. Apply the same validation in order review/quote and submission; different SKUs remain separate.
- Acceptance example: Two lines for the same product with quantities 5 and 5 are rejected, rather than silently merged to 10. A single quantity-10 line uses approved tier/minimum/step/stock rules.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact error response, draft storage/cleanup and client handoff remain open; backend never silently repairs submitted duplicate lines.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend implementation authorized.

## D-PRI-019
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-I answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Reject the change unless the same save explicitly corrects or removes affected tiers."
- Implications: R-PRI-019. A base-price change that invalidates existing tier prices is rejected unless the same authorized save explicitly corrects/removes affected tiers so the resulting schedule is valid. No automatic tier removal or repricing. The combined base/tier save succeeds or fails together; exact concurrency mechanics remain open.
- Acceptance example: Base 100 with tier 90: changing base to 80 alone fails, preserving existing values. A same-save correction to tier 70 or explicit removal can succeed if otherwise valid.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-033/036 settle invalidating quantity-rule changes and forbid baseless tiers; wire/transaction mechanisms require later approval.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend implementation authorized.

## D-PRI-020
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-J answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: Require explicit deactivation first. Deactivation and price removal may happen together with both catalogue and price permissions."
- Implications: R-PRI-020. Block base-price removal from an active product unless it is explicitly deactivated first or in the same operation. Deactivation requires catalogue authority; removal requires price authority; a combined command requires both. Never silently deactivate on a price-only removal or leave a successfully updated active product without a base.
- Acceptance example: Price-only actor cannot remove an active product’s base. Actor with both grants can explicitly deactivate and remove together; restoring a positive base does not automatically activate.
- Remaining/approval boundary: D-PRI-034/036 settle retained buyer prices and no baseless tiers; exact command/idempotency/history conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend implementation authorized.

## D-PRI-021
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-K answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer: "A: One current agreed unit price per buyer organization, without buyer-specific volume tiers."
- Implications: R-PRI-021. Allow at most one current agreed unit price for each vendor product and buyer organization pair. There are no buyer-specific volume tiers; different buyers may have different agreed prices. The price remains positive, uses approved precision and overrides ordinary base/tier pricing as already approved.
- Acceptance example: The same product can have agreed price 80 for buyer A and 85 for buyer B, but cannot have two current prices or a separate buyer-specific tier schedule for buyer A.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Effective dates/history, duplicate write/upsert behavior, connection lifecycle and deletion/fallback remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and VS-03/04/11 verification. No schema, provider or frontend implementation authorized.

- Status: APPROVED — direct user request, recorded as Q-GOV-003 without reconfirmation.

- Implications: R-GOV-007. Use batches of 20, grouped by topic and dependency order, with organization delegated to the assistant. Supersedes the earlier 5–10-question cadence. Preserve stable IDs and existing pending options; recommendations require explicit answers.

## Promotions, pricing lifecycle and initial order rules — 2026-10-13
Batch 24: twenty answers approved. Promotion editing/stopping use the user's B choices; all other selections are A. Business decisions only; no implementation authorization.

## D-PRI-022
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-G answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Independently select product/category scope and buyer scope; both must match."
- Implications: R-PRI-022. Product scope is all products, selected products or selected categories; buyer scope is all eligible or selected buyer organizations. Both dimensions must match. Multiple selections within a dimension match any selected member. Enforce authorized domestic relationships; all does not mean public.
- Acceptance example: Selected category drinks and buyers A/B applies to a drink for A, not another category or buyer C.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Mixed product/category scopes, list bounds, link changes and exact projections remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-023
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-H answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Category promotions follow current category membership."
- Implications: R-PRI-023. Evaluate category eligibility from current product category membership, including after the promotion starts. Moving into/out of the selected category changes subsequent eligibility. This is not an edit of the promotion definition.
- Acceptance example: Moving a product out of a selected category removes that promotion on the next offer evaluation; submitted order terms are not rewritten.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Category deletion/reference cleanup, concurrent changes and snapshot fields remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-024
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-I answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Schedule promotions using dates with exact start/end times."
- Implications: R-PRI-024. Use date-and-time schedule boundaries, supporting sub-day offers rather than whole-day-only schedules.
- Acceptance example: A promotion can run 10:00–11:00 on a chosen date.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Time precision, wire representation, DST ambiguity and invalid local times remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-025
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-J answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Use the vendor organization's country-local timezone, including seasonal changes."
- Implications: R-PRI-025. Promotion schedules follow the vendor country business timezone, not buyer device or deployment timezone. No independent per-vendor timezone setting selected.
- Acceptance example: Clients in different device timezones evaluate the same commercial promotion window.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact country-to-timezone identifiers, correction lifecycle and DST handling remain open; other timezone domains remain Q-LOC-002.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-026
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-K answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Include start; exclude end."
- Implications: R-PRI-026. Promotion eligibility uses start <= evaluation time < end. Consecutive periods do not overlap solely at their shared endpoint.
- Acceptance example: 10:00–11:00 includes 10:00 and excludes 11:00.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Evaluation instant/transaction convention and time precision remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-027
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-L answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Require both a start and an end."
- Implications: R-PRI-027. Every promotion requires a bounded start/end schedule; no open-ended offer selected.
- Acceptance example: Missing end is invalid; the approved exact boundary rule applies to a complete schedule.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Duration bounds, past dates and exact validation errors remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-028
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-M answered B, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "B: Freeze a promotion after it starts; stop and replace it to make changes."
- Implications: R-PRI-028. After its start the promotion definition cannot be edited; authorized changes require stopping it and creating a replacement. Submitted order terms stay intact. Current category membership under D-PRI-023 can still change eligibility without changing the definition.
- Acceptance example: Changing a started fixed price from 20 to 15 is rejected; stop/create a new offer instead. A replacement is a new promotion for tie ordering.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Pre-start edits, stop/replacement atomicity, reference cleanup, audit and retention remain open. An enabled edit permission never bypasses the freeze.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-029
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-N answered B, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "B: Only stop permanently and create a replacement."
- Implications: R-PRI-029. No pause/resume workflow. Explicitly stopped promotions cannot reactivate; create a new promotion if an offer should return.
- Acceptance example: Stop an offer within its schedule: resuming the same identity is denied, including before its original end.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Stop permissions follow promotion authority; exact lifecycle labels, pre-start stopping and history schema remain open.

## D-PRI-032
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-005-Q answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Record no applied promotion if rounding produces no saving."
- Implications: R-PRI-032. A promotion is attributed only when its rounded unit price is strictly less than the no-promotion resolved price. Equality records no promotion; approved promotion-to-promotion tie order remains unchanged.
- Acceptance example: 0.05 with 10% off rounds to 0.05: charge 0.05 and record no applied promotion.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Exact snapshot fields and reporting definitions remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-033
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-L answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Reject invalidating minimum/step edits unless affected tiers are explicitly corrected or removed in the same save."
- Implications: R-PRI-033. Preserve valid thresholds when minimum/step changes. The coordinated save requires catalogue and price authority when both quantity rules and tiers change, and succeeds or fails together. Never silently delete/rewrite tiers.
- Acceptance example: Changing minimum/step to make a threshold invalid alone fails; an explicit valid corrected schedule in the same fully authorized save can succeed.
- Remaining/approval boundary: Concurrency, bulk boundaries and exact DTOs remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

## D-PRI-034
- Status: APPROVED — Q-PRI-001-M answered A, 2026-10-13.
- User answer (faithful option summary): "A: Explicitly remove tiers before or with base removal; retain buyer-specific prices while non-orderable."
- Implications: R-PRI-034. Removing an inactive product's base requires explicit removal of its volume tiers in the same save or beforehand. Retain agreed buyer prices, but they cannot make the baseless product orderable. No dormant baseless tier schedule selected.
- Acceptance example: Base removal with retained tiers fails; explicit tier removal/base removal may succeed, leaving agreed buyer prices stored but unusable until eligible activation.
- Remaining/approval boundary: History retention, restored-base effects on quotes and exact field conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

- Acceptance example: Delete agreed 80 when current ordinary price is 90: next pre-promotion offer is 90, not an ordering block merely for lacking an agreed price.

- Planning effect: Propagated to requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and relevant verification slices. Frontend integration changes remain external handoff.

Ending access never makes that account reusable in another organization. This refines D-ORG-016, not the organization's owner-role transfer.
- Acceptance example: An invitation cannot move an existing organization's user into another organization.
- Remaining boundary: Same-person/email account creation, deactivation/reactivation, exceptional recovery and platform-admin identity representation remain unselected; do not invent alternate-account workarounds.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-019
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-002-L, Clarified answer; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Use role permissions only; remove individual vendor staff edit toggles.
- Implications / R-ORG-019: Vendor editing authority comes from the fixed role; no per-user edit-toggle settings or reset/preservation workflow. Preserve the latest approved enabled catalogue/price/promotion/stock baseline as uniform staff role permissions, with owner/admin management restrictions unchanged. An admin becoming staff receives the staff role. The separate buyer-wide submission restriction remains approved. Supersedes D-ORG-007/012 individual-grant design and dependent D-ORG-008/014 implications.
- Acceptance example: Two active vendor staff users have the same role-derived edit rights; no per-user exception API. Role downgrade removes admin management powers.
- Remaining boundary: Detailed operation fields and in-flight authorization remain open. This does not approve custom/editable role definitions or remove backend authorization checks.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-020
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-E, A after clarification; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Ownership-transfer acceptance expires after seven days.
- Implications / R-ORG-020: Transfer changes the owner role between existing users of the same organization. The original owner stays owner until valid acceptance; expiry leaves ownership unchanged. D-ORG-009's exactly-one-owner and former-owner-becomes-admin rules remain.
- Acceptance example: An expired transfer cannot be accepted or move an account to another organization.
- Remaining boundary: Timestamp precision, request authentication/notification and recovery remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-021
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-F, A after clarification; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Allow one pending ownership-transfer request, revocable or replaceable by the current owner.
- Implications / R-ORG-021: Replacement invalidates the old request; a new request follows the seven-day lifetime. Only eligible existing users of the same organization can accept. Never introduce organization reassignment.
- Acceptance example: Replacing a pending transfer makes the old acceptance link unusable; competing acceptance/replacement cannot create two owners.
- Remaining boundary: Eligible active/suspended recipient states, exact concurrency and exceptional recovery remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-022
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-006-A, Clarified answer; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): No special common-ownership check or restriction.
- Implications / R-ORG-022: Apply normal domestic buyer/vendor connection rules regardless of common legal ownership. Do not add a common-ownership matching requirement or related-party prohibition. Ordinary company fields/deduplication remain independent questions.
- Acceptance example: Separate same-country buyer/vendor records may connect under normal authorization, consent and cap rules without a same-legal-owner check.
- Remaining boundary: Company identifier validation and duplicate-organization policy remain open; no external legal-identity verification provider selected.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-023
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-006-B, A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Either organization's owner/admin, or a platform administrator, may disconnect a trading relationship.
- Implications / R-ORG-023: Staff cannot disconnect. Country/object authorization still applies; global administrative reach is not cap/country bypass.
- Acceptance example: Buyer owner/admin may disconnect a vendor; buyer/vendor staff cannot.
- Remaining boundary: Reason/audit/notification fields and idempotency remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-024
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-006-C, A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Allow disconnection while orders remain open, blocking new trade but preserving existing order completion.
- Implications / R-ORG-024: Block live catalogue/price access and new order submission between disconnected partners. Preserve shared order snapshots/history and allow existing orders and their messages to complete under normal authorization. Disconnection itself neither cancels orders nor credits stock. Only active connections count toward buyer caps.
- Acceptance example: A disconnected vendor can finish an already accepted order; neither partner can use the old connection for new orders.
- Remaining boundary: Suspension/offboarding, exact relationship state/restore races, pending invitations and drafts remain open. Terminal messaging follows D-ORG-026.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-025
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-006-D, A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Retain agreed buyer prices as inactive after disconnection; review/confirm before reuse on reconnection.
- Implications / R-ORG-025: A currently price-authorized vendor member must review/confirm retained agreed terms before they apply again. Normal base/tier prices plus eligible promotions apply meanwhile. Preserve original order prices and require normal compliant reconnection.
- Acceptance example: After reconnection, retained agreed 80 is inactive until confirmed; normal price resolution applies meanwhile.
- Remaining boundary: Per-product/bulk confirmation contract, editing dormant terms and concurrent reconnection remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-026
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-006-E, A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Disconnected partners may read terminal-order conversations but cannot add messages.
- Implications / R-ORG-026: Existing order history remains available to authorized parties. Open-order messaging follows D-ORG-024; terminal orders are read-only while disconnected. Reconnection restores D-ORD-020's connected-partner messaging.
- Acceptance example: A disconnected partner can read a delivered order's thread but sending fails; delivery of an open order ends the disconnected messaging allowance.
- Remaining boundary: Suspension/deletion/retention rules and exact error/projection remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORD-021
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORD-004-G, A; recorded 2026-10-14.

- Implications / R-ORD-021: When the buyer-wide restriction is enabled, staff cannot directly cancel or request cancellation; owner/admin may act subject to timing/reason rules. Under normal mode, current authorized buyer staff retain those routine actions.
- Acceptance example: Enabling restriction blocks subsequent staff cancellation actions across devices without discarding prepared work.
- Remaining boundary: In-flight transaction ordering and exact command/error fields remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORD-022
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORD-004-H, A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): One pending cancellation request per order; an authorized buyer member may withdraw it.
- Implications / R-ORD-022: Current cancellation authority controls withdrawal. Withdrawal appends history and releases the request's dispatch block without cancelling the order or crediting stock. Vendor resolution remains available. Refines D-ORD-013: buyer withdrawal is also a valid way to end pending status.
- Acceptance example: After a valid withdrawal, otherwise valid dispatch may proceed; simultaneous withdrawal/approval must not yield inconsistent outcomes.
- Remaining boundary: Repeat requests after denial, withdrawal reason, detailed concurrency/idempotency remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORD-023
- P0.37 refinement: D-STA-020–025, D-ORD-036–039, D-CAT-081–086 and D-LOC-013–016 resolve report date/access rules, number/replacement lifecycle, stock concurrency/history and language/template behavior. Earlier pending notes apply only where still unresolved.

## D-ORD-024
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORD-006-B, A; recorded 2026-10-14.

- Implications / R-ORD-024: Preserve current order state and historical labels on retirement. Once an order leaves a retired stage it cannot newly enter it. Core meanings and terminal boundaries stay fixed.

## D-ORD-025

## D-BUY-007

- Remaining boundary: Concurrent discard versus save/submit, audit fields and retention remain open.

## D-BUY-008
- Status: APPROVED — Q-BUY-003-E, A; recorded 2026-10-14.

- Implications / R-BUY-008: Discard removes a draft from active work; further preparation requires a new draft. This does not authorize physical erasure or choose audit/data retention.

- Remaining boundary: Retention, internal audit/read visibility and deletion mechanisms remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture, operations and verification traceability updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-048
- P0.33 follow-up: D-CAT-058–070 and D-ORG-031–035 resolve create-only import/preview/confirmation/bounds, image primary/processing/retention and location restoration/required-field choices. Earlier remaining notes below are historical where superseded by those later decisions.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-A, option B; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Products support an ordered gallery of up to five current images.
- Implications / R-CAT-048: Use vendor-owned product media references with a maximum of five attached images and persisted ordering. Supersedes the single-image recommendation; frontend gallery application work is external handoff.
- Acceptance example: Five attached images are allowed; attaching a sixth fails without partially changing the gallery.
- Remaining boundary: Primary-image convention, ordering/replacement contract, reuse and concurrent edits remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-049
- P0.33 follow-up: D-CAT-058–070 and D-ORG-031–035 resolve create-only import/preview/confirmation/bounds, image primary/processing/retention and location restoration/required-field choices. Earlier remaining notes below are historical where superseded by those later decisions.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-B, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Product image uploads accept JPEG, PNG and WebP.
- Implications / R-CAT-049: Other formats, including SVG/arbitrary files, are not approved. Validate actual content under a later-approved processing/security convention; filename/claimed content type alone is not authority.
- Acceptance example: A valid supported-format file is eligible for further validation; unsupported content is rejected.
- Remaining boundary: D-CAT-068/076/077 select metadata removal, animation rejection and input-format preservation; decoder/encoder versions/settings and malformed-file handling remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-050
- P0.33 follow-up: D-CAT-058–070 and D-ORG-031–035 resolve create-only import/preview/confirmation/bounds, image primary/processing/retention and location restoration/required-field choices. Earlier remaining notes below are historical where superseded by those later decisions.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-C, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Each uploaded product image file is limited to 5 MiB (5,242,880 bytes).
- Implications / R-CAT-050: Apply the file-byte limit, not an assumed decoded-pixel limit; oversized uploads fail without attaching media. Transport overhead and pixel/processing limits remain separate.
- Acceptance example: A file of 5,242,880 bytes fits the byte limit; one byte more is rejected.
- Remaining boundary: Dimension/decoded-resource bounds, upload protocol and rate/quota limits remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-051
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-D, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Images are optional for product activation.
- Implications / R-CAT-051: A product meeting the other approved activation requirements may activate with an empty gallery. Attaching/removing an image is not implicit activation/deactivation.
- Acceptance example: A valid product with zero images may be explicitly activated by a role-authorized vendor member.
- Remaining boundary: Other required fields and full activation schema remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-052
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-E, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Historical orders do not freeze product images.
- Implications / R-CAT-052: Text/commercial submission snapshots remain authoritative. An optional historical-order image may use current media only when currently authorized; it cannot bypass disconnected-catalogue restrictions. No order-image snapshot retention requirement is introduced.
- Acceptance example: Replacing a product image does not rewrite frozen product text/prices; disconnected order history cannot expose otherwise unauthorized live media.
- Remaining boundary: Exact historical media projection and referenced-file retention/cleanup remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-027
- P0.33 follow-up: D-CAT-058–070 and D-ORG-031–035 resolve create-only import/preview/confirmation/bounds, image primary/processing/retention and location restoration/required-field choices. Earlier remaining notes below are historical where superseded by those later decisions.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-G, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Order submission requires an active saved delivery location owned by the buyer.
- Implications / R-ORG-027: Reject missing, archived or foreign-buyer delivery references; capture submitted details under the approved snapshot rules. No one-off unsaved delivery-address ordering path selected.
- Acceptance example: A buyer cannot submit using another buyer's location or an archived location.
- Remaining boundary: Exact address/contact fields, archive/submit races and requiredness outside order submission remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-028
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-H, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Vendor fulfillment location is optional on an order.
- Implications / R-ORG-028: Absence does not itself block an otherwise valid order. Any supplied reference still follows vendor ownership/domestic authorization rules. This introduces no warehouse inventory; stock remains vendor-wide.
- Acceptance example: A valid order without a fulfillment-location reference may submit; a foreign vendor location is invalid.
- Remaining boundary: Who assigns it, assignment timing, active-location validation and exact projection remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-029
- P0.33 follow-up: D-CAT-058–070 and D-ORG-031–035 resolve create-only import/preview/confirmation/bounds, image primary/processing/retention and location restoration/required-field choices. Earlier remaining notes below are historical where superseded by those later decisions.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-I, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Locations may be archived while open orders reference them.
- Implications / R-ORG-029: Archive blocks new use, preserves submitted snapshots and lets existing orders finish. Archival alone neither cancels orders nor credits stock. It does not authorize deleting historical data.
- Acceptance example: Archiving a used delivery location leaves open orders intact but prevents new submissions selecting it.
- Remaining boundary: Restoration/deletion, default fallback implementation, exact audit and concurrent use remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-030
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-J, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Archiving a home/default location clears the default without automatic reassignment.
- Implications / R-ORG-030: Where a valid location is required, a subsequent action needs an explicit valid choice; never silently redirect delivery/fulfillment. Home location remains a default, not an access restriction.
- Acceptance example: After home-location archive, an order lacking an explicit valid delivery location fails instead of selecting another address.
- Remaining boundary: Other default types, preference schema and archive/default-write concurrency remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-ORD-031
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORD-005-C, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Submitted delivery addresses/contact details cannot be directly amended before dispatch.
- Implications / R-ORD-031: Changes use authorized cancellation and a new buyer-submitted order with fresh validation. No buyer-request/vendor-approval delivery-correction workflow is selected. Original snapshots remain unchanged; existing cancellation timing still applies.
- Acceptance example: Editing a source address does not change an existing order; a requested delivery change needs the approved cancellation/replacement path.
- Remaining boundary: Exact snapshot fields, replacement linkage and exceptional support/privacy handling remain open; no post-dispatch cancellation permission inferred.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-053
- P0.33 follow-up: D-CAT-058–070 and D-ORG-031–035 resolve create-only import/preview/confirmation/bounds, image primary/processing/retention and location restoration/required-field choices. Earlier remaining notes below are historical where superseded by those later decisions.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-A, option B; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Manual CSV product import is in launch scope.
- Implications / R-CAT-053: Provide backend import contracts, validation/failure results and verification after the detailed import policy is approved. This is distinct from deferred ERP integrations and does not authorize arbitrary import formats or frontend implementation.
- Acceptance example: Launch planning includes CSV import rather than excluding all import capability.
- Remaining boundary: Create/update modes, columns, identities, validation/preview, atomicity, row/file limits and execution model remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-054
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-B, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Bulk product edits are atomic: one invalid change rejects the entire batch.
- Implications / R-CAT-054: No valid subset may be applied from a failed bulk edit. Enforce role/ownership, quota, SKU, pricing and product lifecycle invariants across the resulting batch; no partial persistence.
- Acceptance example: An invalid second item leaves the otherwise-valid first item's previous values unchanged.
- Remaining boundary: Supported bulk operations, batch bounds, import-specific boundary and concurrency/transaction mechanics remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-055
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-001-H, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Case-only changes to a current SKU are allowed as cosmetic edits to the same code.
- Implications / R-CAT-055: Preserve case-insensitive identity and uniqueness. Do not reserve a second previous code for a capitalization-only edit. This cannot reactivate a genuinely different retired code.
- Acceptance example: Current abc-1 may become ABC-1 without a new retired-code entry; an actual previous different code remains unavailable.
- Remaining boundary: Exact trim/case storage, no-op behavior, audit and concurrent edits remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-056
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-F, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Inactive/archived products retain their featured flag but are excluded from buyer featured offers.
- Implications / R-CAT-056: Reevaluate featured eligibility when the product becomes eligible again; retained featuring does not activate the product or bypass ordering/access checks.
- Acceptance example: An archived featured product is excluded from buyer offers; after valid restore/activation its retained flag can apply.
- Remaining boundary: Featured count limit, ordering and exact out-of-stock treatment remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-057
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-G, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): A completed never-attached upload may be cleaned up after 24 hours if no product/history reference exists.
- Implications / R-CAT-057: The cleanup eligibility threshold applies only to never-attached completed uploads. Recheck references and race with attachment so cleanup cannot delete referenced media. This does not set retention for formerly attached files, unfinished uploads or backups.
- Acceptance example: A 23-hour-old unattached upload is not eligible; after 24 hours, a still-unreferenced never-attached upload is eligible.
- Remaining boundary: Completion timestamp precision, scheduling, exact attach/cleanup concurrency and other media-retention classes remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-058
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-C, option B; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): CSV import creates new products only; updates are excluded.
- Implications / R-CAT-058: No update/upsert mode, matching-to-update or blank-cell update behavior. The A answer to conditional Q-CAT-007-D has no effect because its update prerequisite was rejected; Q-CAT-007-F explicitly says no updates. Creation still checks normalized current/archived/retired SKU reservations.
- Acceptance example: An import cannot change an existing product through a matching SKU or backend ID.
- Remaining boundary: Exact create columns, existing-SKU error/report conventions, CSV syntax and preview lifecycle remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-059
- P0.34 refinement: D-CAT-071–080, D-ORG-036–040 and D-LOC-002–006 resolve CSV fields/format/byte bound/preview lifecycle, image animation/output/reference policy, saved contacts/instructions and launch language/preference/fallback choices. Earlier remaining notes below apply only where still unresolved.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-E, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): CSV requires validation preview followed by explicit confirmation; the entire file commits atomically.
- Implications / R-CAT-059: Preview returns proposed creations/errors without catalogue writes. Commit rechecks current permissions, caps, identity/reference/pricing/lifecycle validity; invalid or stale commit changes nothing. Upload/staging storage is not a catalogue mutation.
- Acceptance example: A preview reserves no product slot or SKU; a conflicting creation before confirmation can cause commit to fail with no partial file.
- Remaining boundary: D-CAT-074/075 select 30-minute confirmation and original-result replay. Exact preview IDs/clock boundaries, replay after expiry/retention, stale-result response and execution/transaction mechanism remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-060
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-G, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): CSV cannot set stock counts; use dedicated stock operations.
- Implications / R-CAT-060: Do not accept stock writes from import. Preserve original stock deduction/credit and tracking/reconciliation rules; do not invent zero-count initialization for new products created while tracking is on.
- Acceptance example: A supplied stock-write column cannot initialize or overwrite availability through CSV.
- Remaining boundary: Exact unsupported-column reporting and stock-setup handoff remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-061
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-H, option B; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): CSV may explicitly request activation of new products when all requirements pass.
- Implications / R-CAT-061: An explicit activation field is allowed, not activation inferred from price. Enforce role, unit, price and tracking-dependent stock eligibility. Because CSV excludes stock and new tracked products need stock-authorized completion, imports while tracking is on cannot bypass that prerequisite: import inactive, complete stock separately, then activate.
- Acceptance example: With tracking off, a valid explicit activation may succeed. With tracking on and no initialized count, requested activation fails the atomic import; absence of count must not silently become zero.
- Remaining boundary: Exact field spelling/default representation, activation failure response and full required fields remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-062
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-I, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): CSV unit/category/tag references must already exist for that vendor.
- Implications / R-CAT-062: Reject unknown referenced records instead of creating them implicitly. Apply approved vendor-local normalization/uniqueness and ownership; optional absent references follow the eventual create schema.
- Acceptance example: An unknown category/unit/tag reference yields a row error; no taxonomy or unit side effect is created by preview/import.
- Remaining boundary: Exact CSV reference encoding, archived/reference eligibility and field syntax remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-063
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-J, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): One CSV file may contain at most 1,000 data rows, excluding its header.
- Implications / R-CAT-063: This is an operation bound, not a plan-cap increase. Import must also fit current non-archived product capacity at commit.
- Acceptance example: 1,000 data rows fit the row bound; 1,001 do not. A Starter vendor cannot use this bound to exceed its ten-product cap.
- Remaining boundary: File-byte/cell/line limits, empty-row handling and parser conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-064
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-K, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Duplicate product/SKU rows reject the entire CSV file with duplicate-row errors.
- Implications / R-CAT-064: Create-only import checks vendor SKU identity using approved trim/case comparison. No last-row-wins or silently ignored duplicate rows; no file changes apply.
- Acceptance example: Rows for abc-1 and ABC-1 are duplicates and cannot create two products or silently override each other.
- Remaining boundary: Error field details and reporting of multiple duplicate groups remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-065
- P0.34 refinement: D-CAT-071–080, D-ORG-036–040 and D-LOC-002–006 resolve CSV fields/format/byte bound/preview lifecycle, image animation/output/reference policy, saved contacts/instructions and launch language/preference/fallback choices. Earlier remaining notes below apply only where still unresolved.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-L, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Return row/column validation errors across the bounded file without applying it.
- Implications / R-CAT-065: Do not stop at the first ordinary row error. Unreadable/invalid file structure may prevent row-level analysis and needs a file-level error. Error results do not authorize partial import.
- Acceptance example: A file with bad references in two rows reports both when parsable and changes no products.
- Remaining boundary: Error codes/fields, reporting bounds, format/encoding and processing model remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-066
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-H, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): The first image in the ordered gallery is the primary image.
- Implications / R-CAT-066: No independent primary-image selector. Reordering changes primary identity; empty gallery remains allowed.
- Acceptance example: Move image B to position one: B is primary, without updating a separate primary flag.
- Remaining boundary: Exact ordering/reorder/remove concurrency contract remains open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-067
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-I, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): A formerly attached image with no remaining references may be deleted after 24 hours.
- Implications / R-CAT-067: Measure eligibility from loss of its final reference; recheck all references before deletion. This covers detached files separately from D-CAT-057's never-attached class. Backup retention and actual deletion scheduling remain unselected.
- Acceptance example: A just-detached file is not eligible immediately; after 24 hours with no remaining references it may be deleted. Reattachment prevents deletion while referenced.
- Remaining boundary: Exact detach/re-attach timestamps, cleanup races, logical asset restore and deletion job conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-068
- P0.34 refinement: D-CAT-071–080, D-ORG-036–040 and D-LOC-002–006 resolve CSV fields/format/byte bound/preview lifecycle, image animation/output/reference policy, saved contacts/instructions and launch language/preference/fallback choices. Earlier remaining notes below apply only where still unresolved.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-J, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Normalize visual orientation and remove embedded image metadata, including location/camera data.
- Implications / R-CAT-068: Processed product images must not retain embedded metadata. Preserve intended visual orientation before stripping orientation metadata; do not select a library/output codec implicitly.
- Acceptance example: A rotated camera photo remains visually upright after processing while embedded location/camera metadata is absent.
- Remaining boundary: D-CAT-076/077 select no animation and input-format preservation. Decoder/encoder versions/settings, color handling and processing verification remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-069
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-K, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Decoded images must have each dimension at most 6,000 pixels and at most 20 million total pixels.
- Implications / R-CAT-069: Both decoded bounds and the existing 5 MiB compressed-file limit must hold. Reject oversized images rather than silently accepting them based on compressed size; no automatic resize-to-repair convention selected.
- Acceptance example: 6,000 × 3,000 fits these pixel bounds; 6,000 × 4,000 exceeds 20 million; 6,001 × 1 exceeds the dimension bound.
- Remaining boundary: Animation is rejected under D-CAT-076; decoder resource limits, orientation-evaluation details, detection and validation implementation remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-070
- P0.34 refinement: D-CAT-071–080, D-ORG-036–040 and D-LOC-002–006 resolve CSV fields/format/byte bound/preview lifecycle, image animation/output/reference policy, saved contacts/instructions and launch language/preference/fallback choices. Earlier remaining notes below apply only where still unresolved.
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-L, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): After successful image processing, retain only the processed image and safely remove raw uploaded bytes.
- Implications / R-CAT-070: No retained-original product-media feature. Raw removal follows successful replacement/reference safety; do not remove the only valid image on processing failure. Metadata-stripped processed content is the retained product image.
- Acceptance example: Successful processing leaves the processed asset, not a private raw original containing stripped metadata.
- Remaining boundary: Temporary raw-file lifecycle/failure cleanup, exact successful-processing boundary, backup and derived-size policies remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-031
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-K, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Location names are unique within each organization after trimming and ignoring case.
- Implications / R-ORG-031: Cross-organization name reuse is allowed; IDs remain references. Enforce normalized uniqueness under create/rename/restore concurrency.
- Acceptance example: Two organizations may each use Main warehouse; case/space-equivalent duplicates within one organization fail.
- Remaining boundary: Retained location records remain subject to organization-local naming; deletion/name-release policy, exact normalization/lengths and conflict response remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-032
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-L, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Archived locations can be restored using the same record and ID.
- Implications / R-ORG-032: Restoration does not rewrite historical snapshots or automatically restore cleared home/default selections. Current validity/ownership still apply.
- Acceptance example: Restore a delivery location with its original ID; users whose defaults were cleared do not silently regain it.
- Remaining boundary: Exact naming conflicts, validation, role/audit and restore concurrency remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-ORG-033
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ORG-003-M, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer (faithful summary): Geographic coordinates are optional; a textual location address is sufficient.
- Implications / R-ORG-033: No mandatory coordinate capture or geocoding service. Optional coordinates do not replace required textual address fields.

## D-ORG-034
