## D-ORG-035
- P0.34 refinement: D-CAT-071–080, D-ORG-036–040 and D-LOC-002–006 resolve CSV fields/format/byte bound/preview lifecycle, image animation/output/reference policy, saved contacts/instructions and launch language/preference/fallback choices. - Implications / R-ORG-035: Country follows the owning organization's approved country. Coordinates remain optional and cannot substitute for required textual address fields.

## D-CAT-071
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-M, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer/source: "All answers are A" for all 20 questions in Batch 30; no approval of later questions or implementation.
- Decision (faithful option-A summary): CSV supports SKU, product name, description, unit, pack text, category, tags, minimum, step, base price and explicit activation. Only SKU/name are required for inactive creation; omitted minimum/step default to 1 and omitted activation means inactive.
- Implications / R-CAT-071: Exclude stock, images, volume tiers, buyer-specific prices, promotions and featured flags. Every explicit activation must still pass all approved prerequisites.

- Remaining boundary: Exact headers, references/tag encoding, empty-cell rules and field limits remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-072
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-N, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer/source: "All answers are A" for all 20 questions in Batch 30; no approval of later questions or implementation.
- Decision (faithful option-A summary): CSV uses comma-separated columns and dot-decimal monetary values, UTF-8, a required header, double-quoted fields with escaped double quotes, and no thousands separators.
- Implications / R-CAT-072: Apply approved monetary precision; do not infer separators from a row or organization language.
- Acceptance example: A price value 12.34 uses the approved decimal notation; a comma-decimal value is not silently converted.
- Remaining boundary: Exact header names/order, quoting acceptance, BOM/newline conventions and malformed-input errors remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-074
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-P, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer/source: "All answers are A" for all 20 questions in Batch 30; no approval of later questions or implementation.
- Decision (faithful option-A summary): A validated import preview remains confirmable for 30 minutes.
- Implications / R-CAT-074: Confirmation still revalidates current authorization, capacity, references and business rules. Expired previews require a new preview; expiry does not define erasure or audit retention.
- Acceptance example: An uncommitted preview older than 30 minutes cannot create products.
- Remaining boundary: Exact start/boundary clock, preview identity, result retention and file-erasure policy remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-075
- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-007-Q, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer/source: "All answers are A" for all 20 questions in Batch 30; no approval of later questions or implementation.
- Decision (faithful option-A summary): Repeated confirmation of the same successful preview returns the original committed result without new creations.
- Implications / R-CAT-075: At most one successful commit per preview; a distinct preview needs normal validation. Replay does not grant access after permission loss; exact authorization/result-retention mechanics remain open.
- Acceptance example: A lost success response followed by a permitted retry returns the original created-product result rather than duplicating products.
- Remaining boundary: Concurrent confirmations, replay after preview expiry, actor scope, result retention and wire/error conventions remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

## D-CAT-076

- Planning effect: Requirements, domain/API, architecture/operations and planned verification updated. No implementation authorization.

- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-P, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.

- Status: APPROVED — Q-CAT-008-Q, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- User answer/source: "All answers are A" for all 20 questions in Batch 30; no approval of later questions or implementation.

- Remaining boundary: Exact reviewed-scope/version/clock representation, concurrent catalogue changes and transaction mechanism remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, dependent planning/instructions and planned verification updated; no implementation authorization.

## D-SEC-007

## D-API-009
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-002-E, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- Answer/source: User: "All answers are A", responding to the 20 Batch 39 questions only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-009: Emit money as JSON strings with exactly two decimal places, including trailing zeros. Preserve approved arithmetic and currency rules.
- Acceptance example: An amount of twelve is returned as "12.00", never a floating-point JSON number.
- Remaining boundary: Money maxima, leading-zero input policy and database representation remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

## D-API-010
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-002-F, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- Answer/source: User: "All answers are A", responding to the 20 Batch 39 questions only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-010: Money input strings accept digits with an optional decimal point followed by one or two digits. Reject signs, whitespace, exponents, missing integer parts and empty fractional parts. Field-specific positive/zero rules still apply.
- Acceptance example: "12", "12.3" and "12.34" are valid forms; ".5", "12.", " 12", "+12", "1e2" and excess precision fail.
- Remaining boundary: Leading zeros, length/value maxima and specific validation codes remain open; this grammar does not govern signed stock deltas.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-002-G, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.

- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-011: Represent whole quantities as integer JSON numbers, with no fractional or exponent notation. Apply zero/negative permissions separately to order quantity, minimum/step, stock counts and deltas.
- Acceptance example: Order quantity 5 uses a JSON number; "5", 5.5 and 5e0 do not match the selected quantity grammar.
- Remaining boundary: Safe integer/value maxima, exact lexeme enforcement and database types remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-002-H, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- Answer/source: User: "All answers are A", responding to the 20 Batch 39 questions only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-012: Emit UTC Z timestamps with exactly three fractional digits; accept zero through three fractional digits on UTC Z input and reject greater precision. Date-only fields remain YYYY-MM-DD.
- Acceptance example: 2026-10-14T10:00:00.123Z is valid; an input ending .1234Z exceeds approved precision.
- Remaining boundary: Internal/storage precision, conversion of more precise stored instants, named zones, calendar grammar and exact clock boundaries remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

## D-API-013
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-002-I, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.

- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-013: Accept and return public resource UUIDs only in lowercase hyphenated canonical spelling. UUID v7 remains the resource format; identifiers convey no authorization.
- Acceptance example: An uppercase spelling of a resource UUID is rejected rather than silently normalized.
- Remaining boundary: Generation/library/storage, command-key formats and exact validation errors remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

## D-API-014
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-002-J, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- Answer/source: User: "All answers are A", responding to the 20 Batch 39 questions only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-014: Authorized resource-response schemas emit null for absent optional scalar/object values and empty arrays for empty collections. Do not expose fields excluded by authorization.

- Remaining boundary: Individual schema/projection members remain open; Problem Details extension presence follows its separate contract.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

## D-API-015
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-002-K, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.
- Answer/source: User: "All answers are A", responding to the 20 Batch 39 questions only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-015: Distinguish omitted request fields from explicit null. Omission follows the operation's documented default or unchanged semantics; null clears only explicitly clearable fields.
- Acceptance example: Omitting an editable field does not silently clear it; null for a non-clearable field fails validation.
- Remaining boundary: Create/update field definitions and default values, PATCH format and clearing permissions remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts/model/architecture/operations and planned verification updated; no application code or infrastructure authorization.

## D-API-017

- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-018: Collect independent safely discoverable ordinary field errors after access checks, within an approved bound. Parse/auth failures still stop inappropriate inspection. Existing CSV all-row rules remain.
- Acceptance example: Two independently invalid authorized fields can be reported together without inspecting inaccessible objects.
- Remaining boundary: Error count cap/order, validation phases and detailed CSV compatibility remain open.

## D-API-019
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-003-E, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.

- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-019: Use HTTP 422 for well-formed requests with invalid field values and HTTP 400 for malformed JSON. Other failure categories retain separate mappings.
- Acceptance example: A parsed invalid quantity produces 422; broken JSON syntax produces 400.
- Remaining boundary: Wrong JSON token types, query/path parsing, validation precedence and full status/code mappings remain open.

## D-API-020

- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-020: Use HTTP 409 with a stable specific code for conflicts with current business state. Do not implicitly select conditional-request or ETag behavior.

- Decision (faithful summary) / R-API-022: Do not echo submitted values in validation responses. Return paths/codes and approved safe parameters such as limits; exclude secrets, stack traces and database/provider diagnostics.

## D-API-023
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-004-B, option A; recorded 2026-10-14.

## D-API-029
- Status: APPROVED — revised Q-API-005-A, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "Answer is A", answering only the restated targeted-idempotency question. This is not approval of the original all-writes option A or the remaining Batch 40 questions.
- Decision / R-API-029: Use naturally idempotent state changes and existing resource/action identities where sufficient. Introduce a separate idempotency key only where otherwise the backend cannot distinguish retrying the same intent from a new intended action.
- Implications: No blanket key requirement. Preserve existing replay and exactly-once effect requirements; choosing a mechanism does not waive them. A separate header key is one implementation option, not the definition of idempotency.
- Acceptance example: A repeated favourite-state request can be safe through its existing identity/state rule; a repeated order submission must not create another order or deduct stock twice, while a deliberately new identical order remains possible. Review concrete mechanisms for each operation before implementation.
- Remaining boundary: Exact operation-by-operation mapping, resource/command identity generation, atomic uniqueness/state guards, associated delivery effects, key syntax/scope/lifecycle and all other Batch 40 choices remain open. No provider, library or implementation authorization.
- Planning effect: Requirements, API/domain/architecture/operations and planned verification updated. Scope remains backend/infrastructure planning; no MFA added.

## D-API-030
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-M, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-030: Separate idempotency keys identify direct new-order submissions without a draft, stock delta adjustments, new order messages and explicit invitation resends. Favourites, shared-draft submission, CSV preview confirmation, order transitions/cancellation and ordinary edits use existing identities/state/revision protection instead.
- Acceptance example: Retrying a direct submission with the same key creates no second order or stock deduction; a deliberately new identical order uses a new intent/key.
- Remaining boundary: This is the approved initial mapping only. Other creates/actions, endpoint schemas, exact uniqueness/state guards and result lookup/retention for natural identities remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-035
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-F, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-035: A retained key reused with different command content returns 409 and causes no new write.
- Acceptance example: A completed stock increase cannot be changed into a larger increase by reusing its key.
- Remaining boundary: Exact code/schema, request validation order and matching/canonicalization implementation remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-036
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-G, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-036: A duplicate request while the original is running returns 409 with a distinct in-progress code; retry later with the same key. Never execute both.
- Acceptance example: Concurrent submissions of one intent do not both create orders; the duplicate receives the in-progress outcome.
- Remaining boundary: Exact code, retry timing, durable claim/lease ownership and crash recovery remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-037
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-H, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-037: Retain the replayable successful response for seven days from command completion. This does not delete business records/history or used-key metadata.
- Acceptance example: A currently authorized matching retry within the window can recover its successful command response.
- Remaining boundary: Exact expiry equality, cleanup/retention controls, privacy and result metadata storage remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-038
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-I, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-038: Reject a known completed key after its replayable result expires and require checking business state. Preserve enough used-key metadata to recognize it; result expiry alone cannot make that key reusable.
- Acceptance example: A known eight-day-old completed key cannot silently create another order after its stored response expires.
- Remaining boundary: Used-key metadata retention/privacy/deletion, lookup contract, restore behavior and exact expired-replay response remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-039
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-J, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-039: Within the replay window return the original success status and business response body after current authorization checks; generate fresh per-request correlation/security headers.
- Acceptance example: A retry returns what its command produced even if the resource later changed; loss of access prevents disclosure.
- Remaining boundary: Exact stored business-header/body scope, redaction, role/projection changes and request-ID placement remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-040
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-K, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-040: Retain completed successes only. After definitive failure with no committed effect, release the claim so corrected input can reuse the key. An in-progress or uncertain outcome must not be released or re-executed blindly.
- Acceptance example: A definitively rejected command can be corrected; a timeout with unknown commit outcome is reconciled instead of assumed failed.
- Remaining boundary: Failure classification, claim transaction/cleanup and unknown-outcome reconciliation remain open; successful-key mismatch protection still applies.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-041
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-005-L, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-041: Commit the business change, applicable successful replay record and required durable notification/job intent in one PostgreSQL transaction.
- Acceptance example: A committed order cannot lack its required replay record or delivery intent because of separate-commit failure.
- Remaining boundary: Schema/locking, transaction boundaries for natural identities/large jobs and failure recovery remain open; external provider delivery is outside the transaction.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-042
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-006-A, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-042: Deliver business-triggered email/push and similar provider work through durable workers after database commit. No provider network call occurs while holding the business transaction.
- Acceptance example: Provider delay does not hold the order transaction open; durable work remains after a successful business commit.
- Remaining boundary: Worker leases/retry bounds/provider ambiguity remain open. Invitation failed-delivery resend remains manual; initial queued delivery is not an automatic resend. Login-provider exchanges are separate.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-API-043
- Status: APPROVED — Q-API-007-A, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-API-043: Expose an opaque revision string with resource reads and accept expectedRevision in edit commands. Existing stale protections remain mandatory; ETag/If-Match is not the selected edit contract.

## D-ENG-031
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ENG-005-F, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-ENG-031: Use PostgreSQL Read Committed as the default business-transaction isolation level, with reviewed explicit locks/constraints for each invariant. Database defaults alone are insufficient.
- Acceptance example: Concurrent stock/quota/ownership/key operations cannot violate invariants merely because Read Committed allows concurrency.

- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-ENG-032
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ENG-005-G, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-ENG-032: Limit each ordinary business database lock-acquisition wait to at most two seconds. This is not the total request deadline.
- Acceptance example: A blocked lock acquisition cannot wait five seconds under the ordinary business policy.
- Remaining boundary: Configuration scope, transient response, request deadline and separate migration/worker limits remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

## D-ENG-033
- Status: APPROVED — Q-ENG-005-H, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-ENG-033: Retry database deadlock or serialization failures internally at most three total attempts, rerunning authorization and business checks each time. Do not blindly retry validation/stale-input failures or unknown commit outcomes.
- Acceptance example: A transient deadlock may be retried, but a newly removed permission can make the next attempt fail authorization.
- Remaining boundary: Backoff/jitter, total deadline, error classification and interaction with idempotency claim/commit recovery remain open.
- Planning effect: Requirements, contracts, architecture/domain/infrastructure constraints and planned verification updated. Historical remaining-boundary notes are resolved only as explicitly specified here.

- Status: APPROVED — Q-SEC-006-A, option A; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Answer/source: User: "All asnwer are A", applying to Batch 41 only. Later questions and implementation remain unapproved.
- Decision / R-SEC-039: Serialize final write authorization with relevant account deactivation or permission reduction; commit order determines the outcome. Revocation first blocks the write; a write committed first remains valid.

## P0.48 — delegated planning and AI-led operating model
User instruction: "For the rest of all of the question you can choose your recommendation, just keep in mind this is vibe coded and in future development and devops, 99% of work will be done by AI."

This grants decision-making authority for remaining choices. It is not a claim that every historical proposal or unspecified setting has already been selected. D-GOV-005 supersedes the interview/individual-confirmation portions of D-GOV-000/004 and R-GOV-002/007; the scope, implementation gate and existing confirmed business rules are preserved.

## D-GOV-005
- Status: APPROVED — direct user instruction; Q-GOV-004; recorded 2026-10-15.
- Source: User instruction quoted in the P0.48 section; faithful interpretation of explicit delegation/operating expectation.
- Decision / R-GOV-008: The assistant may choose and record its recommendations for remaining project choices without routine user confirmation. This supersedes the ask-every-choice policy and automatic 20-question interviews. Apply existing approved requirements; record each concrete delegated choice, rationale, implications and verification rather than treating all historical proposals as automatically selected.
- Rationale / acceptance example: A future schema, dependency, naming or operational convention can be researched and selected in the records without another approval questionnaire.
- Boundary: The mandate does not authorize implementation, spending, deployment, external messaging or changes to already-set business requirements. Actual owner/budget/domain/legal facts cannot be fabricated. Ask only for indispensable missing facts, irreconcilable conflicts with explicit requirements or an action requiring separate authority.
- Planning effect: Governing instructions, requirement/verification records and operating guide updated; no implementation authority inferred.

## D-GOV-007
- Status: APPROVED — direct user instruction, 2026-10-24.
- User answer: "The backend code is now ready, your job is to complete the frontend implementation, specifically: remove any mocked layers; link up all the frontend code with the actual backend; amend any types, ui and usages based on the actual backend; if anything crucial is missing, add a note document listing every gap; then refactor the whole frontend to be fully optimized, scalable and production ready; finally, run everything and fully test every frontend screen and its api, fixing any gaps or issues you find."
- Decision: Supersedes the D-GOV-002 restriction that frontend application source is read-only evidence. The assistant now also owns web (FE/apps/web), mobile (FE/apps/mobile), shared frontend packages (FE/packages) and frontend browser tests (FE/e2e) integration with the verified local backend.
- Boundary: OpenAPI and backend rules remain the wire/business authority; frontend mocks are removed rather than used as backend design input. Provider purchases/provisioning, store releases, PR merges, production deployments and external messages keep their separate approval boundaries. Real Google/Apple/FusionAuth/FCM/R2 client acceptance (X/G gates) is still external and is not claimed by local testing.
- Implications: FE/CLAUDE.md "build against the mocked service layer" guidance is historical. Frontend gaps found against the backend are recorded in FE/BACKEND_GAPS.md.

- Decision / R-GOV-009: Treat the user’s expectation that AI will perform approximately 99% of future development and DevOps as an operating-design constraint. Optimize for simple, explicit, reproducible, observable and recoverable backend/infrastructure workflows with durable repository guidance.
- Rationale / acceptance example: A new AI session can discover decisions, run the approved checks and follow documented release/recovery steps without needing prior chat memory.

- Planning effect: Governing instructions, requirement/verification records and operating guide updated; no implementation authority inferred.

## Recording future answers
Use stable D-<topic>-<number> IDs and linked question IDs. Record direct user answers distinctly from assistant selections under D-GOV-005; include date, authority, rationale, affected requirement/design/API/plan IDs, work released and superseded decisions. A partial answer resolves only its explicit portions. Split compound questions into sub-IDs when needed. Deferrals need blocked work and a revisit trigger. Product scope answers are recorded above; their unresolved details must not be filled with assumed defaults.

## D-PLAN-007
- Login-email implementation refinement,2026-10-18: [email implementation](../backend/identity/login-email-change.md#implementation) adds expected Account revision as the natural request identity, global current/reserved/in-flight email claims coordinated with setup, independent purpose-encrypted mailbox proofs and a narrow own-operation status/reconciliation exception to the credential barrier. Completion requires both proofs plus fresh same-account authority; all sessions are revoked after success. Unknown operations freeze the target beyond request expiry until positively confirmed; no remote write replay. Provider administration/conformance, actual mail delivery and exceptional recovery remain separately gated. These are delegated backend refinements, not new user answers.
- Password lifecycle refinement,2026-10-18: [implementation](../backend/identity/password.md#implementation) specifies latest-only hashed reset proofs, separately encrypted delivery intents, exact30-minute expiry/3-address-and20-source-hour limits, committed operation barriers, immediate session retirement and atomic mandatory PASSWORD_CHANGED producers. A successful signed-in change retains current session identity/absolute deadline and rotates secret/CSRF; reset retires all sessions. No success-secret replay or blind provider-write retry; positive read-only reconciliation precedes fresh password verification. Definite rejection releases the barrier without restoring retired sessions. These are delegated implementation choices, not additional user answers.
- Password reauthentication refinement,2026-10-17: [implementation](../backend/identity/reauth.md#implementation) binds provider verification to the current session account, snapshots and rechecks email/binding/epoch/generation around an out-of-transaction provider call, and rotates only the current session without extending expiry/activity or evicting another session. Wrong password shares login throttling; provider failure cannot rotate. Immediate old-secret invalidation means a lost successful response requires ordinary login; no authentication-secret replay/grace is added. Default activation and live conformance gates remain.
- HTTP implementation refinement,2026-10-17: [authentication HTTP record](../backend/identity/auth-http.md#implementation) specifies five-minute cookie-bound pre-auth nonces, stable HMAC-derived session CSRF proofs with digest-only persistence, explicit disabled-by-default wiring, strict request/error mapping, own-session HTTP commands, rolling admission and cleanup. Schema5 revokes old browser sessions to migrate CSRF representation. This supersedes the earlier independent-random-CSRF generation detail while preserving approved hash-only storage, cookie/Origin defenses and no MFA. Real provider/hosting/client gates remain; see [verification](../backend/identity/auth-http.md#verification).
- Subsequent implementation refinement,2026-10-17: [login boundary](../backend/identity/login.md#implementation) records short database transactions around provider verification, durable HMAC throttle admission/idempotent failure completion, credential-operation barrier enforcement and standalone browser/native transport policy. Full HTTP CSRF/Origin/CORS wiring and credential-operation orchestration remain unfinished; [verification](../backend/identity/login.md#verification) records51 passing tests and current artifacts.
- Implementation refinement,2026-10-17: [identity implementation](../backend/identity/identity-core.md#implementation) records the internal session lock protocol,60-second server-only verified-password proof lifetime, hashed secrets, exact expiry/caps/rotation and restricted runtime grants. HTTP/provider integration remains gated; these internal services do not enable login or change the no-MFA decision. See [executed verification](../backend/identity/identity-core.md#verification).
- Status: SELECTED UNDER USER DELEGATION — D-GOV-005; 2026-10-15; P1.0 review candidate (amended by D-PLAN-025 in P1.1).
- Topic: Session and proof mechanisms. Resolves design portions of Q-SEC-002, Q-SEC-003, Q-SEC-005, Q-SEC-006.
- Decision / R-PLAN-007: Select hashed opaque secrets, cookie/CSRF/native transport, rotation, latest proof/email-claim coordination and bounded throttles as specified; no application MFA.
- Rationale / alternative: Keeps authorization/session lifecycle local while using managed credential verification.
- Exact specification: [DESIGN_SPEC.md](../architecture/design-spec.md), 4. Required behavior and cross-field rules there are part of this selection.
- Verification: T-R-PLAN-007 in [TRACEABILITY.md](traceability.md); empirical gates and external inputs in [PLANNING_REVIEW.md](planning-review.md). No test pass or implementation approval inferred.
